4. **Plan expiry** (OLD 408, NEW 535-542): NEW adds `max(0.0, -early_s)`, which is 0.0 because `early_s &gt;= 0` without a landing quantile. Bitwise equal over 2e6 random draws.
5. **`tick`** (OLD 451-499, NEW 615-666): `queue_len` now comes from `_queue_ahead()` (NEW 633, 668-674), which returns `len(waiting_queue)` here. It is evaluated before the queued-plan loop instead of after; the loop does not change the queue. This is the only moved evaluation on the path.
6. **`read_deadline`** (OLD 865-882, NEW 1297-1318): the new branch `if self._expected_cost or plan.early_s &lt; 0.0` (NEW 1309-1312) is false and falls through to the old return.
7. **`_register_next_use`** (OLD 974-996, NEW 1410-1433): `registry.forget(previous)` became `registry.move(previous, tail)` (OLD 988, NEW 1424). `move` (`next_use_eviction.py` NEW 100-107) pops the same entry and also records it in `_moved`, which only `ReturnChanceStrategy` reads. Registrations at commit, arrival (`next_use=now`), session end, abort and expiry are unchanged, and the `nue_register` line has the same fields.
8. **`_expire`** (OLD 1047, NEW 1484-1485): `_end_session` is now behind `_forecast_ends_session`, which is True.
9. **`unified_radix_cache.py` `_launch_storage_hit`** (OLD 2923-3044, NEW 2942-3071): two `_report_room_refusal` calls, on a refused read (NEW 3021) and a shrunk read (NEW 3054-3055). Both are no-ops while `room_refusals is None`; the refusal, shrink, revoke and `poll_miss` statements are unchanged.
10. **`unified_radix_cache.py` `_init_storage_read_scheduling`** (NEW 599-602): computes `host_room_floor_tokens`, read only by `_follow_room`.
11. **`arg_groups/hicache_hook.py`** (NEW 223-254, 280-319): validation only. Nothing raises for these values, and the resolved `retry_poll_interval=0` is unchanged.
12. **`managers/scheduler.py`** (NEW 1436-1439): passes the four new values.
13. **Imports:** `next_turn_policy.py` is stdlib and msgspec only, with no import-time side effects.

Run from `agent_cache/`: `nice -n 10 python3 -I -B frozen/oracle_scripts_staging/c26_ec/stall_ec.py [arms...] | tee results/campaign26/stall_ec.out`; `STALL_CSV=path` also writes per-arm parts. Default arms `jit_exact ec_r13 ec_r1 nofc`. Core:
     ```python
     ENTRY = re.compile(r"ReqTimeStats\(rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+), .*queue_duration=([\d.]+)ms.*entry_time=([\d.]+)")
     IO = re.compile(r"HICACHE_EVT s2h_io rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+) pages=(\d+) tokens=\d+ ms=(\d+)")
     LANDED = 0.05; SLACK = 0.1; BOUND = 0.1
     def stalls(run):
         """Per turn: (stall s, of it inside a read s, queue wait s, reads that ended after admission)."""
         ...
         for k, (arrive, wait) in entry.items():
             admit = arrive + wait
             mine = [(end, ms) for end, ms in io.get(k, ()) if end <= admit + SLACK]
             stall = min(max(max((end for end, _ in mine), default=arrive) - arrive, 0.0), wait)
             inside = sum(max(0.0, min(end, admit) - max(end - ms, arrive)) for end, ms in mine)
             out[k] = (stall, min(inside, stall), wait, len(io.get(k, ())) - len(mine))
     ```

4. Errors and fixes:

The follow-up check (`holds.py`) showed four in five of the held turns waited 3.5–5.5 s more for the GPU after their read landed; turns admitted as their held read landed carry only 0.006–0.019 s per turn. @@ 26329 2026-11-12T02:33:03.229Z A len=2972
**Running now:** `nofc_rep`, the repeat of the rival that uses no gap forecast (it reads from SSD only when a turn arrives and evicts ended sessions first, queued turns last), at 44 users and 70 GB. It is at minute 102 of 120 with about 10,300 turns served, mean TTFT 0.88 s so far and no errors, and ends about 02:51 UTC. The no-hint rival at 128 users and 160 GB starts right after and runs to about 04:55 UTC.

**What the results say so far (44 users, 70 GB, x10 gaps):**

| Arm | Mean TTFT, 6,715 shared returning turns | Mean turn latency |
|---|---|---|
| Original HiCache (two runs) | 5.47, 6.62 s | 25.8, 29.9 s |
| Old hint-free arm, read pins only (two runs) | 1.61, 1.89 s | 15.8 s (one run) |
| JIT p50, today's method (three full runs) | 1.35, 1.48, 1.60 s | 15.3, 15.9 s (two runs) |
| No hints, queue state only | 0.71 s | 11.6 s |
| Rules 1 and 3, three-quantile hints (two runs) | 0.66, 0.78 s | 11.8, 12.2 s |
| JIT, exact hints (one run from 1 November) | 0.51 s | 11.5 s |

1. **Most of the gain needs no hint.** An engine that only uses queue state covers 96 % of the way from original HiCache to exact hints. "Queue state" here means reading at arrival, keeping queued turns' history, the arrival gate and hold, and re-reads. Those mechanisms are worth about 1.0 s over the old hint-free arm.
2. **JIT p50 loses because of when it reads, not what it evicts.** Its early reads sit in host memory waiting for their turn: they need about 91K tokens where only 44K is free. Changing only the read start (rule 1 alone) brings it to 0.67 s (one run).
3. **Rules 1 and 3 are not separated from no hints.** The arm moved 0.12 s between its two runs, more than its lead. What does repeat is 23 to 25 % fewer SSD pages read.
4. **Exact hints are about 0.2 s ahead on TTFT, but not on latency or throughput.** Among the leading arms a turn takes 12.0 to 12.5 s and 11,500 to 11,700 turns are served; neither moves with hints. The exact-hint number is a single old run; a rerun on this build is queued.
5. **Why hints have little to win here.** The free host room holds two or three histories and the SSD read thread is busy only 16 to 21 % of the time. All differences between the leading arms sit in two load episodes, where the GPU queue decides and a rerun of the same arm moves 0.1 to 0.25 s.
6. **The measurements hold up.** An independent recomputation from the raw logs matches every table, and neither the new engine build nor the new GPU unit moves JIT p50.

**What this means for the paper:** at this setting the forecast buys SSD traffic, not latency, so "forecasts drive the gain" does not hold here. The baseline of record should be the no-hint, queue-aware engine. Still open are the learned price and soft pins (queued) and the 128-user rival (next); the 128-user rival decides whether any measured setting supports the hint. Two settings where the design could matter have not been run: x70 gaps at 128 users, and a larger host pool at 44 users.

@@ 26376 2026-11-12T02:39:54.105Z A len=2152
Half right: the mechanism is as you say, but a larger pool does not give *more* gain, because it also removes most of the reads there are to start early.

- **Short queue, read at arrival:** outside the load episodes a turn that has to read from SSD has a median TTFT of 1.18 s with no hints, against 0.34 s for a turn that reads nothing. So the read costs about 1 s when nothing else is in the way.
- **Short queue, early read timed right:** with exact hints the median reading turn is at 0.34 s, the same as a turn that read nothing. So starting the load early does remove the cost.
- **But few turns read:** only 5 to 10 % of turns read there, so the gain is about 0.06 s per turn on average. It shows in the tail of TTFT, not in the mean or in throughput.
- **A larger pool cuts the reads:** live sessions hold a median of 1,176K tokens (1,443K at most).
  - A 70 GB pool holds 712K, with 44K free beyond the GPU's copy.
  - A 100 GB pool holds 1,017K, with 349K free. About a third as much history sits on SSD, and there is room to start all of those reads early.
  - A 160 GB pool holds 1,628K, more than the sessions ever hold, so nothing is on SSD and there is nothing to load early.

So more room makes early loading safe, but the total gain is roughly the number of reads times that 1 s, and the number of reads falls as the pool grows. The setting where early loading should pay most is one with many reads, a short queue and room all at once: x70 gaps at 128 users and 160 GB. That one is not queued.

I appended three arms at 100 GB and 44 users to the end of the queue to test your point directly: no early reads, early reads from the p50 (today's JIT p50), and early reads from exact hints. If room is what JIT p50 lacks, it should close on exact hints there. They would start late tonight; tell me if you want them moved up to run right after the 128-user rival, or dropped.

**Timing:**
- The current arm (`nofc_rep`) ends about 02:51 UTC, roughly 10 minutes from now.
- The no-hint rival at 128 users then runs 2 h, to about 04:55 UTC.
- The whole queue, 12 more arms including the three new ones, ends about 03:30 UTC on 13 November.

     ```python
     for f in (line.split() for line in open(os.path.join(RES, "campaign26", "manifest.txt"))):
         # Arms of another setting (128 users, another host pool) join only when named.
         if (f[0].endswith("_c128") or re.search(r"_h\d+$", f[0])) and f[0] not in sys.argv[1:]:
             continue
         RUNS[f[0]] = f[1]
         DESC[f[0]] = ...
     EXTRA = os.path.join(RES, "campaign26", "extra_runs.txt")
     for f in (line.split(None, 2) for line in (open(EXTRA) if os.path.exists(EXTRA) else ()) if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")):
         RUNS[f[0]] = f[1]
         if f[0] in sys.argv[1:]:
             DESC[f[0]] = f[2].strip()
     ```

# Data Visualization

A chart is **read by people and executed by you**. This skill turns "make it look
good" into a procedure with checks, so the result is right by construction rather
than by taste.

**The method here is design-system-agnostic.** Nothing in the procedure, the form
heuristic, the six checks, or the mark specs is specific to one product. A design
system supplies a small set of *parameters* (its ramps, a categorical order, a
diverging pair, a status palette, a texture, its surfaces, its filter components);
the method consumes them unchanged. A **validated default palette** is the
reference instance, fully specified in `references/palette.md`. To target your
brand, read that file's structure and substitute its values - touch nothing else.

> The single most important habit: **the color part is computable, so compute it.**
> Never eyeball whether a palette is colorblind-safe - run `scripts/validate_palette.js`.

## The procedure - do these in order

Color comes LAST. Most bad charts pick colors first.

1. **Pick the form.** What is the data's job - magnitude, identity, polarity, a
   single headline, change-over-time? The job picks the chart type, and sometimes
   the answer is *not a chart* (a stat tile or hero number). -> `references/choosing-a-form.md`
2. **Assign color by the job it does.** Categorical (identity), sequential
   (magnitude), diverging (polarity), or status (state) - each has one rule.
   Assign categorical hues in fixed order, never cycled. -> `references/color-formula.md`
3. **VALIDATE the palette - run the script, don't reason about Delta E.**
   `node scripts/validate_palette.js "<hex,hex,...>" --mode light` (relative to
   this skill's base directory - or load it as `<script type="module">` in the
   chart's own page, where it reads
   `data-palette` off `<body>` and logs a `console.table` report). It returns
   pass/fail on the lightness band, chroma floor, adjacent-pair CVD separation,
   the normal-vision floor, and contrast. Fix anything that FAILs before continuing. Re-run for
   `--mode dark` with that mode's surface.
4. **Apply mark specs & spacers.** Thin marks, 4px rounded data-ends anchored to
   the baseline, 2px lines, >=8px markers, a 2px surface gap between fills (stacked
   segments and adjacent bars alike) and a 2px surface ring on overlapping marks,
   selective direct labels. -> `references/marks-and-anatomy.md`
5. **Add the hover layer - by default.** An HTML/SVG chart *is* interactive; ship
   a crosshair+tooltip on line/area and a per-mark hover tooltip on bar/dot/cell.
   The only form that skips it is a bare stat tile with no plot. Hit targets bigger
   than the mark; filters in one row above the charts. -> `references/interaction.md`
6. **Final accessibility pass.** For >= 2 series a legend is always present and <= 4
   are also direct-labeled (a single series needs no legend box - the title names
   it), so identity is never color-alone; a table view exists; dark mode is **selected** - its own
   steps from the same ramps, validated against the dark surface, not an automatic
   flip; texture is available for the CVD/print/forced-colors case.
7. **Render it and look at it.** The validator checks color, not layout - open or
   screenshot the output and eyeball it for label collisions, geometry, and overflow
   before calling it done.

Then check the result against **`references/anti-patterns.md`** - it is the catalog
of what goes wrong. If your chart matches an entry, it's wrong.

## Non-negotiables (true in every design system)

- **Assign categorical hues in fixed order, never cycled.** A 9th series is never a
  generated hue - it folds into "Other," small multiples, or composite encoding.
- **One axis.** Never a dual-axis chart (two y-scales). Two measures of different
  scale -> two charts, small multiples, or indexed to a common base. *(This is the
  #1 chart mistake - see anti-patterns.)*
- **Color follows the entity, never its rank.** A filter that changes the series
  count must not repaint the survivors.
- **Sequential = one hue, light->dark. Diverging = two hues + a neutral gray
  midpoint.** Never a rainbow; never a hue at the diverging midpoint.
- **Run the validator before shipping any categorical palette.** CVD Delta E >= 8 is the
  target (OKLab ×100); 6-8 is a floor that is legal ONLY with secondary encoding. A
  normal-vision floor below 15 is a hard FAIL - full-color readers can't tell the
  pair apart; re-step it on the adjacent pairlist (secondary encoding does not excuse
  this one); under `--pairs all` cut series or facet instead - see check 4. A contrast WARN
  obligates visible labels or a table view - it is not dismissable.
- **Thin marks; a legend always present for >= 2 series (none for one), with
  selective direct labels (never a number on every point); recessive grid/axes.**
  One exception, legend kept: the tall segmented bar (*Figures* in `references/marks-and-anatomy.md`).
- **Text wears text tokens, never the series color** - values, labels, and legends
  stay in primary/secondary/muted ink; a colored mark beside them carries identity.
- **Status colors are reserved** (good/warning/serious/critical) and never reused
  for "series 4"; they ship with an icon + label, never color alone.

## Plugging in a design system

The method is invariant; only these parameters change per system. The reference
instance - every value filled in - is `references/palette.md`.

| Parameter | What the system provides |
|---|---|
| **Ramps** | the hue scales (named steps) the palette draws from |
| **Categorical theme** | the fixed hue order (a named theme); default + alternates |
| **Sequential hue** | the default single hue for magnitude |
| **Diverging pair** | two warm/cool poles + a neutral midpoint |
| **Status palette** | good / warning / serious / critical - steps distinct from categorical |
| **Texture fill** | one directional hand-drawn fill, used at 45° / 135° |
| **Surfaces** | light & dark chart-surface colors (the validator needs these) |
| **Filter controls** | date-range & dimension controls (behavioral spec in `interaction.md`) |

To onboard a new system: fill those rows, feed its ramps to the validator, and let
it snap each slot to the nearest passing step. Structure and rules stay as written.

## Reference files

| File | What it answers |
|------|-----------------|
| `references/choosing-a-form.md` | Which chart type / is it even a chart? |
| `references/color-formula.md` | The four jobs, the six checks, snap-to-passing |
| `references/marks-and-anatomy.md` | Mark specs, spacers, labels, figures, hero number |
| `references/interaction.md` | Tooltips & hover, filters & time ranges |
| `references/components.md` | The pieces a chart is made of - build each in plain HTML |
| `references/anti-patterns.md` | **What goes wrong - check every chart against this** |
| `references/palette.md` | **The reference palette instance** - every parameter, filled in; swap for your brand's |
| `scripts/validate_palette.js` | Runnable six-checks validator (run it; don't eyeball) |

@@ 27371 2026-11-12T17:12:12.173Z A len=2116
Yes, both are implemented, and both are in the frozen build the campaign is running; neither has a finished two-hour run yet.

**Rule 2 (soft pins)**
- **What it does:** when a turn that has already arrived cannot find host room for its read, the engine releases early reads' pins, least likely to return first, until the shortfall is covered.
- **Where:** `--hicache-storage-next-turn-soft-pins on`; the logic is `soft_pin_victims` at `python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_turn_prefetch.py:1207`, called from `python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/unified_radix_cache.py:2997`.
- **Status:** unit-tested only so far. Its first full run is the all-four-rules arm, due about 6:53 PM ET.
- **Known gap:** a turn that reached the scheduler within the last scheduling pass can have its already-landed early read released. Closing that needs a scheduler hook, which I have not added.

**Rule 4 (learned price)**
- **What it does:** updates the price of host memory every 5 seconds from what the room showed.
- **Where:** `--hicache-storage-next-turn-price-learning on|harm`; the controller is `PriceController` at `python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_turn_policy.py:218`.
- **Two variants:** `on` is the rule as the deck states it (price follows refused room minus idle room). `harm` sets the price to the delay early reads caused other turns, per token-second held.
- **Which one the arms use:** `harm`. Replaying the deck's rule on the logged signals of the rules-1-and-3 run sent the price to its floor within a minute, and it sat there for 77 % of the run. The deck's rule is still in the engine for comparison.
- **Status:** running now for the first time in a full arm (`ec_r134`, ends about 12:42 PM ET). The price has updated 1,070 times so far, starting at 5e-5 and ranging from 5.7e-7 to 7.5e-5.
- **Not built:** the per-key recalibration part of rule 4. The closest thing is an eviction option that learns each key's gaps from the gaps the engine has seen, and no queued arm uses it.

The code is uncommitted, as before. So the "rules 1 to 4" series for the new figures exists once the all-four-rules arm finishes this evening.

@@ 27560 2026-11-12T17:35:22.161Z A len=62

@@ 27583 2026-11-12T17:36:18.368Z A len=3515
Yes: the logs show the largest remaining loss is not about when to fetch, and I think the stronger design follows from that rather than from more rules.

**Where the best arm's time still goes** (exact hints on the new build, whole run, returning turns; mean TTFT 0.82 s, median 0.27 s):

| Part | Mean | Share |
|---|---|---|
| Before the scheduler has the request | 0.14 s | 17 % |
| Scheduler queue | 0.38 s | 47 % |
| After admission | 0.29 s | 36 % |

**What we still miss**

1. **Recompute of history that was on SSD.** About 1 % of returning turns (123) recompute their history at about 11 s each. That is 14 % of all TTFT seconds and 23 % of all prefill work, mostly inside the load episodes.
   - **Cause:** in 58 of them the read was refused host room, and in 25 the data was read and then lost before admission.
   - **Why room is short:** the host pool keeps a copy of everything on the GPU, so only 44K of its 712K tokens are free, while a refused read needs about 20K.
   - **Hints don't change it:** the no-hint arm shows the same pattern (113 turns).
   - **Rule 2 likely won't either:** the no-hint arm has no early reads to release, yet 50 of its turns were refused.
2. **Per-turn overhead before the scheduler.** It grows with prompt length, from 0.04 s under 8K tokens to 0.17 s over 64K. I have not profiled it, but the server re-tokenizes the whole history each turn with one tokenizer worker.
3. **GPU memory in the episodes.** Fourteen minutes hold 89 % of the queue seconds, with the GPU pool 80–96 % full. Going from about 20 to 28 running requests adds about 11 % throughput while holding 40 % more of the pool.

**A better design**

- **One copy per page (my recommendation).** A page that is on the GPU and already saved to SSD needs no host copy; host memory then holds only sessions that are off the GPU, and a restore passes straight through to the GPU. In-memory capacity goes from 712K to 1,380K tokens, against a median live context of 1,176K. The forecasts would then decide which sessions get 712K of host memory instead of 44K. Your paper plan already lists the mirror as open item 4, blocked by the write-back lookup bug. The 100 GB arms (about 1–7 AM ET tomorrow) are the first test.
- **Use forecasts in aggregate (more novel, unproven).** Per-session gap forecasts bought at most 0.2 s, but their sum across sessions is much steadier, and the episodes hit every arm at the same minutes. Predicting occupancy 30–60 s ahead would let the engine act before the room fills, making rule 4 predictive rather than reactive. Agent-level admission that reacts to cache pressure already exists (CONCUR, MARS, ThunderAgent), so the novelty needs a proper check; I only ran a quick search and read snippets.
- **What I would not pursue:** finer fetch timing. Its ceiling here is about 0.2 s and the literature on it is crowded.

These breakdowns come from one run per arm on one workload, and the capacity gain is arithmetic, not a measurement. Details are in the last entry of `agent_cache/results/campaign26/DECISIONS.md`.

Decisions for you:
- Should I test the aggregate-forecast idea offline on the existing logs? It needs no GPU and does not touch the queue.
- Do you want an arm at about 136 GB, which equals the no-mirror capacity? I'd wait for the 100 GB results first.

Sources:
- [CONCUR (arXiv 2601.22705)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.22705)
- [MARS (arXiv 2604.26963)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.26963)
- [ThunderAgent (arXiv 2602.13692)](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.13692)

@@ 27761 2026-11-12T18:00:36.835Z A len=68
Found the mechanism in the price log; writing it down and answering.

@@ 27766 2026-11-12T18:00:59.338Z A len=2517
On TTFT it is not established that it is worse: 0.80 s equals the repeat of rules 1 and 3 (0.78 s), and only the first run of rules 1 and 3 (0.66 s) is clearly ahead. Its behaviour does differ from both fixed-price runs, though, and in the harmful direction, because the learned price is lowest exactly when room is about to run out.

**What happened**

- **The price falls during calm stretches.** With no harm to charge, it decayed to about 3e-6 just before the first load episode and to 2e-6–6e-6 before the second, roughly a fifteenth of the fixed 5e-5.
- **So the engine filled the room with early reads going into each episode.** In the six minutes before the first episode it held 17–35K tokens of early reads, against 0–2.5K under the fixed price. Refusals started in those minutes, before the episode proper.
- **The price recovered only after the damage.** It was back near 5e-5 a few minutes into each episode, once reads had already been refused.

**Net effect over the run, against the two fixed-price runs**

| | Rules 1+3, fixed price | Rules 1+3+4, learned price |
|---|---|---|
| Turns recomputing 4K+ tokens of history | 136, 148 | 215 |
| Of those, read refused room | 72, 74 | 111 |
| History recomputed | 2.8M, 3.1M tokens | 4.0M tokens |
| Reads landed before arrival | 130, 134 | 206 |
| Wait for the SSD read alone, per turn | 0.09, 0.10 s | 0.07 s |

It bought about 75 more reads landing early, and paid with about 70 more turns recomputing roughly 18K tokens each.

**Why the rule behaves this way**

- **It prices the average, not the margin.** The price is set to the measured harm per token-second that early reads hold. That ratio stayed about the same (2.7e-5) even though harm doubled and held memory nearly tripled, so nothing pushes the price back up when the engine over-stages.
- **The room's value is two-sided.** Outside the episodes about 80K tokens are free and room is nearly worthless; inside them about 25K are free and it is very expensive. A two-minute average sits between the two and lags both.
- **The deck's own form of rule 4 would go further the same way.** Replayed on the fixed-price run's log, it dropped to its floor within a minute.

The price for this room should follow its current or forecast state, not the recent past. That is the aggregate-forecast idea from my earlier answer, and it is now the specific fix I would try for rule 4. This is one run of the learned-price arm; details are in the last entry of `agent_cache/results/campaign26/DECISIONS.md`.

5. Problem Solving:

Caveats stated: one run per arm, capacity gain is arithmetic, novelty only checked by a quick search.
   - **Why rules 1+3+4 does no better than 1+3 (analysis complete, recorded, NOT yet told to the user):** on TTFT it equals the 1+3 repeat (0.80 vs 0.78), so "worse" is not shown. The learned price sits below 5e-5 in 76 % of updates (median 3.1e-5) and decays to 3e-6 at minutes 48–51 and 2e-6..6e-6 at minutes 78–87, i.e. it is lowest just before each load episode and recovers to ~5e-5 only by minutes 54–57 and 90–91, after the refusals. So the arm enters episodes with the room taken by early reads: staged pins at minutes 46–51 of 17K, 35K, 22K tokens vs 0.1K, 0.0K, 2.5K under the fixed price; refusals begin there (204K, 94K, 432K vs 0, 0, 18K). Whole run vs the two fixed-price runs: 215 turns recomputing 4K+ tokens (136, 148), 111 with the read refused room (72, 74), 4.0M tokens recomputed = 33 % of prefill (2.8M, 3.1M), refused launches 654 (395, 420), harm charged 1,081 s (457, 529), staged pins mean 5.7K (2.2K, 1.9K); what it bought: 206 reads landed before arrival (130, 134) and 0.07 s/turn waiting for the read alone (0.09, 0.10). The harm rule prices average harm per staged token-second (2.7e-5 here vs 2.9e-5 and 3.9e-5), a ratio that does not rise as staging triples, so nothing stops the price from falling when the room is idle (80K tokens free outside minutes 52–64 and 85–92, 25K inside). The deck's dual-ascent form would fall to its floor (open-loop replay). A price for this room must depend on the room's current or forecast state, not on the last two minutes.

     ```python
             "designs": dict(out="compare_c26_designs", ref="jit_p50_d", extra=("oracle_v2",), fixed={"baseline": BASELINE}, limit=None, ...),
             # HiCache and the oracle are campaign 19's runs, the JIT arms campaign 23's on the build before; one run per arm.
             "designs_c128": dict(out="compare_c26_designs_c128", ref="jit_p50_c128", extra=(), limit=6900.0, labels=("ec_r134_c128", "ec_full_c128"),
                                  fixed={"baseline_c128": "20261018_103544_three_tier_wc_NAT160", "oracle_v2_c128": "20261018_154254_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_NAT160",
                                         "jit_p50_c128": "20261102_023031_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned_NAT160",
                                         "jit_exact_c128": "20261102_002657_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned_NAT160"},
                                  what={"baseline_c128": "original HiCache", "oracle_v2_c128": "old oracle v2, exact hints", "jit_p50_c128": "JIT, p50 hints",
                                        "jit_exact_c128": "JIT, exact hints", "ec_r134_c128": "rules 1, 3 and 4, learned price", "ec_full_c128": "all four rules"},
                                  core={"baseline_c128": "#2a78d6", "oracle_v2_c128": "#4a3aa7", "jit_p50_c128": "#a0307a", "jit_exact_c128": "#1baf7a",
                                        "ec_r134_c128": "#eb6834", "ec_full_c128": "#eb6834"},
                                  dash={"ec_r134_c128": (0, (5, 1.5, 1, 1.5)), "ec_full_c128": (0, (1, 1.5))})}
     OUT, REF, CORE, LIMIT = os.environ.get("FIGSET_OUT") or os.path.join(RES, SET["out"]), SET["ref"], SET["core"], SET["limit"]

     def head_view(cl, sv, t_to):
         """The client and server records cut to the turns sent, and the events stamped, before t_to."""
         turns = [t for t in cl["turns"] if t["t0"] < t_to]
         rids = {t["rid"] for t in turns}
         before = lambda xs, key: [x for x in xs if x[key] < t_to]
         return dict(cl, turns=turns, caps=before(cl["caps"], "t")), dict(
             sv, batches=before(sv["batches"], "t"), d2h=before(sv["d2h"], "t0"), h2d=before(sv["h2d"], "t0"),
             h2s={op_id: op for op_id, op in sv["h2s"].items() if op.get("t_submit", t_to) < t_to},
             s2h={rid: op for rid, op in sv["s2h"].items() if rid in rids},
             s2h_staged={rid: op for rid, op in sv["s2h_staged"].items() if min(op.get("t_start", t_to), op.get("t_io_end", t_to)) < t_to},
             ntp=before(sv["ntp"], "t"), evict_device=before(sv["evict_device"], "t"), evict_host=before(sv["evict_host"], "t"),
             load_back_init=before(sv["load_back_init"], "t"), posts=[t for t in sv["posts"] if t < t_to],
             host_moves=[move for move in sv["host_moves"] if move[0] < t_to])
     ...
         if LIMIT:
             # compare_c128.py's cut: a 2 h run also loses the 300 s before its own last send.
             cl, sv = head_view(cl, sv, min(LIMIT, max(t["t0"] for t in cl["turns"]) - 300))
     ```

@@ 28676 2026-11-12T22:00:04.095Z A len=3036
The learned-price arm at 128 users finished clean at 4:48 PM ET, and the 128-user figure set is drawn in `agent_cache/results/compare_c26_designs_c128/`. The all-four-rules arm at 44 users started right after with soft pins on and should end about 6:53 PM ET.

## What the 128-user arm shows

**The pricing rule never started a read early.** All 3,670 start decisions during the run were "leave the read to the turn's arrival". The rule expected a queue wait of about 29 s against a read of about 5 s, so an early start buys nothing. Three things follow:

- **The learned price never moved.** Nothing was read early, no read was refused room, and the price stayed at 5e-5 for the whole run.
- **Reads were timed like just-in-time reads.** After arrival the engine holds each read so it lands just before admission, the same as in the JIT arms.
- **The same rule did read early at 44 users.** There it started 838 reads early, at moments when it expected no queue wait, so one rule covers both settings without a switch.

**Results, first 6,900 s of each run** (one run per arm):

| | Original HiCache | Old oracle | JIT exact | JIT p50 | No hints | Rules 1+3+4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Turns | 8,334 | 15,525 | 15,384 | 15,366 | 15,418 | 15,502 |
| Mean TTFT | 50.20 s | 20.58 s | 20.93 s | 20.98 s | 20.84 s | 20.57 s |
| SSD pages read | 433K | 1,438K | 998K | 1,195K | 1,153K | 1,049K |

- **TTFT:** the new arm is level with the old oracle. The five leading arms span 20.57–20.98 s and there is no repeat at 128 users, so no order among them is shown.
- **SSD traffic:** 27 % under the old oracle, 12 % under JIT p50, 9 % under the no-hint arm, 5 % over JIT exact.
- **What it tests:** with rules 1 and 4 idle, this arm is effectively rule 3 (eviction by return chance) on top of arrival-time reads.

## Figures

The set has the same figures as the 44-user one, for original HiCache, the old oracle, JIT p50, JIT exact and rules 1+3+4. Start with `pricing_vs_jit.png` and `ec_wait_split.png`.

- **Cut to two hours:** the HiCache and old-oracle runs lasted five hours, so every run is cut to its first two.
- **Older build for JIT:** the two JIT runs are from the earlier build (campaign 23), not this campaign's.
- **Fourth panel:** `pricing_vs_jit.png` shows SSD read rate instead of host memory, since the room is not the limit at 128 users.
- **Footnotes:** `pricing_vs_jit.png` and `ec_wait_split.png` say that the pricing arm started no read early.

The 44-user figures still redraw byte-identical after the script changes. The record is in `results/campaign26/DECISIONS.md`.

     ```python
     def started_early(run):
         """Did rule 1 start a read before its turn arrived? From the run's ec_start lines."""
         with open(os.path.join(RES, run, "server.log"), errors="replace") as f:
             return any("HICACHE_EVT ec_start" in line and "decision=now" in line for line in f)
     ...
         if priced and not any(started_early(a.run) for a in arms if a.key.startswith("ec_")):
             notes.append("Here rule 1 started no read before its turn arrived: the queue wait is longer than the read, so it left each read to arrival, "
     ```

   - **`agent_cache/results/campaign26/checks/read_starts.py`** (NEW, kept check) and **`checks/read_starts.out`**:
     ```python
     """When a pricing arm starts its SSD reads: rule 1's start decisions, the price log and the hold on arrived turns' reads.
     From agent_cache/:  nice -n 10 python3 -I -B results/campaign26/checks/read_starts.py label=run [label=run ...]
     Per run, from server.log's ec_start, ec_price and ntp_hold lines: the decisions by kind (now: read started before its
     turn arrived; wait: timed for later; arrival: left to the turn's arrival) with the estimates they were taken on; the
     price's range with the most tokens read pins held, staged and refused and the harm charged; the reads held after
     arrival. Times are seconds since the client started. A run without pricing prints its holds only."""
     import datetime as dt, json, os, re, sys

     TS = re.compile(r"^\[(.{23})\] HICACHE_EVT (ec_start|ec_price|ntp_hold) (.*)")
     stamp = lambda s: dt.datetime.strptime(s, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S.%f").replace(tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc).timestamp()
     q = lambda v, p: sorted(v)[min(len(v) - 1, int(p * len(v)))]
     fields = lambda text: dict(part.split("=", 1) for part in text.split() if "=" in part)

     for name, run in (a.split("=") for a in sys.argv[1:]):
         client = sorted(d for d in os.listdir(f"results/{run}") if d.startswith("client_"))[-1]
         rows = (json.loads(line) for line in open(f"results/{run}/{client}/client.jsonl") if line.strip())
         t_start = next(r["t_start"] for r in rows if r.get("kind") == "run")
         starts, prices, holds = [], [], []
         for line in open(f"results/{run}/server.log", errors="replace"):
             m = TS.match(line)
             if m:
                 {"ec_start": starts, "ec_price": prices, "ntp_hold": holds}[m.group(2)].append((stamp(m.group(1)) - t_start, fields(m.group(3))))
         print(f"{name} {run}")
         ... (prints per decision kind: count, time range, read and queue-wait p10/p50/p90, "read shorter than the wait in N";
              price range, most tokens held/staged/refused, harm charged, room at the end; holds: count, held p10/p50/p90, expected wait p50, load p50/p90)
     ```

@@ 29150 2026-11-13T00:06:52.865Z A len=2959
The all-four-rules arm finished clean at 6:53 PM ET, and the 44-user figures in `agent_cache/results/compare_c26_designs/` are redrawn with it as a seventh series (dotted orange).

**Result: all four rules reach 0.66 s mean TTFT, the level of the best rules 1+3 run, but read 27 % more from SSD than rules 1+3+4.** By the repeat rule nothing new is shown on TTFT, since this arm has one run.

| 6,715 shared returning turns | JIT exact | Rules 1+3 | Rules 1+3+4 | All four rules | No hints |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mean TTFT | 0.51, 0.54 s | 0.66, 0.78 s | 0.80 s | 0.66 s | 0.71, 0.72 s |
| SSD pages, whole run | 388K, 397K | 437K, 415K | 420K | 534K | 562K, 559K |
| Reads staged before arrival | 1,202, 1,207 | 629, 560 | 838 | 1,569 | 0 |

Two numbers in a cell are two runs of that arm.

## What rule 2 did

- **It works as built.** An arrived turn's read now takes room from early reads instead of being refused. The delay charged to early reads fell from 1,081 s to 404 s, the learned price settled at about a quarter of its starting value, and early reads nearly doubled.
- **The cost is wasted reads.** 462 of the 1,569 early reads were given up for an arrived turn, and nearly all of those turns read again. The reads given up had loaded 14 % of everything the run read from SSD.
- **The SSD saving mostly goes.** Rules 1+3 read 22–26 % fewer pages than the no-hint arm; all four rules read 5 % fewer.
- **No more reads were ready at arrival.** 196 reading turns found their read landed, against 206 without rule 2.

## How to read the TTFT

- **Against rules 1+3+4:** 0.14 s faster, one run each. A rerun of rules 1+3 alone moved 0.12 s, and the gain is not on the turns rule 2 touched, so it is not shown.
- **Against no hints:** ahead in both pairs by 0.05–0.06 s; not shown without a repeat.
- **Against JIT exact:** behind in both pairs by 0.12–0.15 s.
- **Latency and throughput:** level with the best arms (12.03 s per turn, 11,706 turns by minute 115).

## Figures

Open `pricing_vs_jit.png`, `ec_wait_split.png` and `timeline_ec_full.png` first. With seven entries the legend of `pricing_vs_jit.png` ran off the right edge, so the script now picks the column count that fits (two here). The other figure sets still redraw byte-identical.

## For you to decide

At 70 GB only 44K tokens of host memory are free beyond the GPU's copy, and rules 2 and 4 only move that room between early reads and arrived turns. Whether they pay off with more room is untested: the three 100 GB arms in the queue are the no-hint arm and the two JIT arms.

I recommend adding an all-four-rules arm at 100 GB at the end of the queue, about 2 h more, ending around 9:20 AM ET Friday. I have not touched the queue.

The next arm (`fq90`, just-in-time with landing quantile 0.9) started at 6:54 PM ET, is running clean, and ends about 8:56 PM ET. The record is in `results/campaign26/DECISIONS.md`, with the new check in `results/campaign26/checks/bumps.py`.

   - **Engine flags:** `--hicache-storage-next-turn-read-timing {just_in_time,expected_cost,arrival}`, `--hicache-storage-next-turn-memory-price` (5e-5), `--hicache-storage-next-turn-price-learning {off,on,harm}`, `--hicache-storage-next-turn-soft-pins {off,on}`, `--hicache-storage-next-turn-landing-quantile` (fq90: 0.9), `--radix-eviction-policy return_chance` with `{"horizon_s":30}` / `{"score":"lru"}` / `{"score":"queue"}`; arrival gate `on` in all `owned` arms. Engines: `engine_ec_20261111d` (all rules), `engine_ec_20261111e` (d + score `queue`), `engine_owned_20261101`, `engine_5df71c4bf0_v2`.
   - **Rule 2 in the engine (`soft_pin_victims` in `frozen/engine_ec_20261111d/python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_turn_prefetch.py`, lines 1207-1252):** when the read of a queued (arrived) turn needs room, staged reads of turns not yet arrived are given up, least likely back within their own read time first (`chance = plan.dist.chance_within(idle_s, horizon_s=read seconds)`); each bump calls `self._price.refuse(tokens)` and `self._price.charge(chance * read_seconds)` and logs `HICACHE_EVT ec_bump rid=<bumped turn> for_rid=<arrived turn> tokens=N chance=C`. `_release_staged` logs `ec_release` (pin given back when the entry has passed). Rule 4 `_follow_room` logs `ec_price price refused idle excess room held staged harm_ms`.
   - **Rule 1 decision log (`ec_start`):** `decision=arrival` (left to arrival), `now` (started before arrival), `wait` (timed for later); one line per change of decision. A bump resets `plan.decision = ""`.
   - **Arm labels (44 users):** `baseline`/`base_rep`, `hintfree`/`hintfree_ctl`, `jit_p50`/`jit_p50_ctl`/`jit_p50_d`, `jit_exact`/`jit_exact_d`, `oracle_v2`, `ec_r1`, `ec_r13`/`ec_r13_rep`, `ec_r134`, `ec_full` (DONE), `fq90` (RUNNING), `ec_r3` (queued), `nofc`/`nofc_rep`, `nofc_noend`/`nofc_noend_rep`. Runs: `ec_full` = `20261112_214834_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned_custom_L1nat_H70` (client `client_215131_cmp_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned`); `ec_r134` = `20261112_153600_..._H70`; `ec_r13` = `20261111_070012_..._H70`; `ec_r13_rep` = `20261111_224624_..._H70`; `ec_r1` = `20261111_110558_..._H70`; `fq90` = `20261112_235315_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned_custom_L1nat_H70` (client `client_235446_...`). 128-user labels as before (`ec_r134_c128` = `20261112_194449_three_tier_wc_oracle_v2_nu_owned_NAT160`, etc.).
   - **Rule for reading repeats (pre-registered):** a difference between two arms counts as shown when every pair of their runs has the same sign and the smallest difference exceeds the largest move between two runs of either arm. `ec_full`, `ec_r134`, `ec_r1` have one run each.

     ```python
     import matplotlib.pyplot as plt  # noqa: E402
     from matplotlib.lines import Line2D  # noqa: E402
     from matplotlib.ticker import FuncFormatter, NullLocator  # noqa: E402
     ...
     NCOL = 3  # legend columns at most; fewer when the labels would run past the figure's right edge
     LEGEND = dict(frameon=False, fontsize=9.5, handlelength=1.8, columnspacing=1.6)
     WIDTH_IN, LEFT, DPI = 8.6, 0.085, 180  # figure width; where the title, legend, panels and footnote start, as a share of it; PNG dpi
     WRAP = 132  # footnote characters per line at this width and 8 pt
     ...
     def legend_columns(labels):
         """The most columns, up to NCOL, at which the legend ends inside the figure; measured on a scratch figure."""
         for ncol in range(NCOL, 1, -1):
             # Same width, anchor and dpi as the saved figure, so the box ends where the real legend will.
             fig = plt.figure(figsize=(WIDTH_IN, 1), dpi=DPI)
             box = fig.legend([Line2D([], [])] * len(labels), row_major(labels, ncol), loc="upper left", bbox_to_anchor=(LEFT, 1), ncol=ncol, **LEGEND)
             fig.canvas.draw()
             fits = box.get_window_extent().x1 <= fig.bbox.x1
             plt.close(fig)
             if fits:
                 return ncol
         return 1
     ...
         ncol = legend_columns([a.label for a in arms])
         legend_rows = -(-len(arms) // ncol)
         top_in, bottom_in = 0.85 + 0.22 * legend_rows, 0.62 + 0.165 * len(lines)
         height = PANEL_IN * len(args.panels) + top_in + bottom_in
         fig, axes = plt.subplots(len(args.panels), 1, figsize=(WIDTH_IN, height), sharex=True, facecolor=jv.SURFACE, squeeze=False)
     ...
         fig.text(LEFT, 1 - 0.14 / height, args.title or figset["title"], fontsize=12, color=jv.INK, va="top", weight="semibold")
         fig.legend(row_major(handles, ncol), row_major(labels, ncol), loc="upper left", bbox_to_anchor=(LEFT, 1 - 0.42 / height), ncol=ncol, labelcolor=jv.INK,
                    **LEGEND)
         jv.footnote(fig, LEFT, "\n".join(lines))
         fig.subplots_adjust(top=1 - top_in / height, bottom=bottom_in / height, left=LEFT, right=0.86, hspace=0.42)
     ...
             fig.savefig(os.path.join(out, f"{args.name}.{ext}"), dpi=DPI, facecolor=jv.SURFACE)
     ```

   - **`agent_cache/results/campaign26/checks/bumps.py`** (NEW kept check) and **`checks/bumps.out`**:
     ```python
     """Rule 2 (soft pins): what the bumped staged reads cost, and what the turns they made room for got.
     From agent_cache/:  nice -n 10 python3 -I -B results/campaign26/checks/bumps.py label=run [reference=run]
     An ec_bump line is a staged read given up so that the read of a turn that has arrived finds room. For the first run:
     the bumps and the SSD pages their reads had loaded, when the bumped turns arrived and whether they read again, and the
     SSD pages by what became of the read. With a reference run (the same arm without soft pins): TTFT of the bumped
     turns, of the turns the room went to and of the other returning turns, beside the same turns there.
     Times are seconds; a turn is a (user, turn) pair, and only turns both runs recorded are set side by side."""
     import collections, datetime as dt, json, os, re, statistics as st, sys

     EVT = re.compile(r"^\[(.{23})\] HICACHE_EVT (ec_bump|s2h_io|ntp_arrive|ntp_stage) rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+)(.*)")
     FOR = re.compile(r"for_rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+) tokens=(\d+) chance=([\d.]+)")
     PAGES = re.compile(r"pages=(\d+)")
     stamp = lambda s: dt.datetime.strptime(s, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S.%f").replace(tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc).timestamp()
     q = lambda v, p: sorted(v)[min(len(v) - 1, int(p * len(v)))] if v else float("nan")
     three = lambda v: f"{q(v, .1):.1f} {q(v, .5):.1f} {q(v, .9):.1f}"

     def turns(run):
         """(user, turn) -> the client's record, for the turns that got a first token."""
         client = sorted(d for d in os.listdir(f"results/{run}") if d.startswith("client_"))[-1]
         rows = (json.loads(line) for line in open(f"results/{run}/{client}/client.jsonl") if line.strip())
         return {(r["conv"], r["turn"]): r for r in rows if r.get("kind") == "turn" and r.get("ttft") is not None}

     def beside(name, keys, own, other):
         """Mean TTFT of the keys in the run, and against the reference on the keys both have."""
         ... (prints count, mean and median TTFT; on keys in both runs: means, paired mean, faster/slower by over 0.05 s)

     (name, run), ref = sys.argv[1].split("="), (sys.argv[2].split("=") if len(sys.argv) > 2 else None)
     ... (parses ec_bump, s2h_io, ntp_arrive, ntp_stage; prints: bumps of staged reads, turns, arrived turns served, tokens, chance p10/p50/p90;
          pages the bumped reads had loaded and how long the data sat; bump-to-arrival times and what followed
          (read again before arrival / read at arrival / no further read / never arrived); SSD pages by read fate
          (at or after arrival / staged and kept / staged, then bumped); then beside() for bumped turns, turns the room went to, other returning turns)
     ```

5. Problem Solving:
   - **`ec_full` (44 users, 70 GB, all four rules; done 6:53 PM ET, clean):** 12,161 turns in 2 h, 0 client errors, 0 tracebacks, settings as queued. On the 6,715 shared returning turns 0.66 s: level with the best pricing run (rules 1+3 0.66/0.78, rule 1 0.67), ahead of rules 1+3+4's one run (0.80) by 0.14 s, ahead of the no-hint rival (0.71/0.72) by 0.05-0.06 s, behind JIT exact (0.51/0.54) by 0.12-0.15 s. By the repeat rule nothing new is shown on TTFT (one run; a rerun of rules 1+3 moved 0.12 s), and the gain over `ec_r134` does not sit on the turns the bumps touched (bumped turns +0.06 s, turns the room went to +0.18 s, other returning turns -0.12 s).
   - **Rule 2 works as built:** harm charged to early reads fell from 1,081 s (`ec_r134`) to 404 s; the learned price settled lower (median 1.4e-5 against 3.1e-5); staged reads rose from 838 to 1,569.
   - **Its cost:** 462 staged reads bumped (29 % of staged reads, 47 % of staged pages, 14 % of all pages read); SSD pages 534K = 27 % over `ec_r134`, 22-29 % over rules 1+3, only 5 % under the no-hint rival (fixed-price rules were 22-26 % under); share of SSD reads used 72 % (88-89 % other pricing arms); reads per reading turn 1.44; no more turns found their read landed at arrival (196 of 1,049 against 206 of 988).
   - **Latency/throughput:** 12.03 s per turn and 11,706 turns before minute 115, level with the best arms (`ec_r134` 12.67 s, 11,446).

@@ 29522 2026-11-13T02:07:32.937Z A len=2327
`fq90` finished clean at 8:56 PM ET with 0.94 s mean TTFT on the shared turns: well ahead of JIT p50, but behind every leading run.

This arm is JIT p50 with one change: each read is timed to finish at the 90th percentile of the forecast gap (late) instead of the 10th (early). It has one run, so nothing counts as shown by the repeat rule.

| 6,715 shared returning turns | JIT p50 (early) | `fq90` (late) | Rule 1 alone | No hints | JIT exact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mean TTFT | 1.35–1.60 s | 0.94 s | 0.67 s | 0.71, 0.72 s | 0.51, 0.54 s |
| Host memory held by early reads, of 44K free tokens | 24.6K | 13.2K | 2.7K | 0 | 4.0K |
| Recomputed tokens per turn | 928–1,007 | 446 | 181 | 135–145 | 120–151 |
| Turns sent by minute 115 | 10,043–10,208 | 11,204 | 11,580 | 11,645–11,680 | 11,699–11,703 |

The memory row comes from one run per arm.

- **Against JIT p50:** 0.41–0.66 s faster in all three pairs, where the three JIT p50 runs differ by up to 0.25 s among themselves.
- **Against the leading runs:** behind all eleven, by 0.14–0.29 s (0.40–0.43 s for exact hints). On the wider 10,830 turns it shares with them alone, the gaps shrink to 0.05–0.26 s, and its gap to rule 1 shrinks from 0.27 s to 0.09 s.
- **Why it trails rule 1:** it finishes about as many reads before their turn arrives (419 against 367), but they wait much longer for it, 60 s against 12 s at the 90th percentile. They therefore hold five times the host memory, so more reads of arrived turns are refused room (625 against 396) and more history is recomputed.
- **What it means for the design:** this is the deck's argument for rule 1 observed in a run. A fixed percentile cannot tell a turn about to arrive from one a minute away; starting by expected cost can. The edge over this fixed percentile is 0.09–0.27 s on one run each, and it changes nothing against the arms that start no read early.

`ec_r3` (rule 3 alone) started at 8:57 PM ET, is running clean and ends about 10:59 PM ET. I will redraw `compare_c26_designs` when it lands; `fq90` is not part of that figure set. The queue is unchanged and ends about 7:15 AM ET Friday.

The record is in `results/campaign26/DECISIONS.md`, with the new memory check in `results/campaign26/checks/pin_hold.py`. Still open from the last report: whether to add an all-four-rules arm at 100 GB.

   - **Landing quantile in the engine (`frozen/engine_ec_20261111d/python/sglang/srt/mem_cache/next_turn_prefetch.py`):** in `_resolve_hint`, `early_s = entry.p50 - dist.quantile(self._landing_quantile)` when the flag is set (negative for 0.9: the read lands at the gap's p90), else `early_s = max(0.0, entry.p50 - entry.p10)` (the base JIT p50 arm lands at the p10). Negative `early_s` is handled on purpose: plan `expires_at = predicted + max(0.0, -hint.early_s) + max(MIN_EXPIRY_S, hint.next_gap_s)`; `read_deadline` returns `max(deadline - min(0.0, plan.early_s), now + NTP_JIT_MARGIN_S)` ("Started on purpose past the p50, so it stays a timed read"); `trigger_time(admission_at, load_s, margin_s=plan.early_s + NTP_JIT_MARGIN_S)`. The flag requires `just_in_time` timing (validated in `hicache_hook.py`).
   - **Unpin reason `abort`:** in JIT arms it is the plan timing out (`_expire`: "Drop a plan whose turn never arrived", logs `ntp_expire ... reason=timeout`; fq90 had 98 with `staged=1`, 38 with `staged=0`; `jit_p50_d` 103 / 76); in pricing arms it is rule 1's release (`ec_release`; `ec_r1` has no `ntp_expire` lines) or a rule-2 bump (`ec_bump`).
   - **Pin events:** `srs_pin rid=... tokens=N node=... pinned=LEVEL` is logged when a read lands; `srs_unpin rid=... tokens=N reason={admit,abort,replaced} pinned=LEVEL`; `ntp_arrive rid=...` marks the turn's arrival; `ntp_stage` a read staged before arrival; `srs_dispatch ... launched={0,1}` (0 = refused room); compare_ec's "mean pinned tokens" is the time-weighted mean of the `pinned=` level over the kept part of the run.

     ```python
     """Host memory held by read pins, split by whether the read landed before its turn arrived.
     From agent_cache/:  nice -n 10 python3 -I -B results/campaign26/checks/pin_hold.py label=run [label=run ...]
     Per run, from server.log's srs_pin, srs_unpin and ntp_arrive lines: every pin is paired with the next unpin of the same
     turn and counted as tokens x seconds held. An early pin is one held over EARLY s before its turn arrived (to its unpin
     when the turn never came); its hold is split at the arrival into the wait for the turn and the wait in the queue. The
     mean level is all token-seconds over the span from the first to the last pin event, and each part's share of it is
     printed in tokens. Times are seconds; quantiles are p10 p50 p90."""
     import collections, datetime as dt, re, sys

     EVT = re.compile(r"^\[(.{23})\] HICACHE_EVT (srs_pin|srs_unpin|ntp_arrive) rid=\S+?-c(\d+)-t(\d+) (.*)")
     TOKENS = re.compile(r"tokens=(\d+)")
     REASON = re.compile(r"reason=(\w+)")
     stamp = lambda s: dt.datetime.strptime(s, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S.%f").replace(tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc).timestamp()
     q = lambda v, p: sorted(v)[min(len(v) - 1, int(p * len(v)))] if v else float("nan")
     three = lambda v: f"{q(v, .1):.1f} {q(v, .5):.1f} {q(v, .9):.1f}"
     EARLY = 0.05  # s; a read at arrival logs its pin within a few ms of the arrival line, on either side

     for name, run in (a.split("=") for a in sys.argv[1:]):
         arrive, open_pins, holds, first, last = {}, {}, [], None, None
         for line in open(f"results/{run}/server.log", errors="replace"):
             m = EVT.match(line)
             if not m:
                 continue
             t, kind, key = stamp(m.group(1)), m.group(2), (int(m.group(3)), int(m.group(4)))
             if kind == "ntp_arrive":
                 arrive[key] = t
                 continue
             first, last = first or t, t
             if kind == "srs_pin":
                 open_pins[key] = (t, int(TOKENS.search(m.group(5)).group(1)))
             elif key in open_pins:
                 at, tokens = open_pins.pop(key)
                 holds.append((key, at, t, tokens, REASON.search(m.group(5)).group(1)))
         span = last - first
         # Seconds a pin was held before its turn arrived; negative when it was taken after the arrival.
         wait = lambda h: min(h[2], arrive.get(h[0], h[2])) - h[1]
         early = [h for h in holds if wait(h) > EARLY]
         late = [h for h in holds if wait(h) <= EARLY]
         area = lambda hs: sum(tokens * (end - at) for _, at, end, tokens, _ in hs)
         before = sum(h[3] * wait(h) for h in early)
         waits = [wait(h) for h in early]
         reasons = collections.Counter(h[4] for h in early)
         print(f"{name} {run}")
         print(f"  pins {len(holds)} paired ({len(open_pins)} still held at the end), mean level {area(holds) / span:,.0f} tokens over {span / 60:.0f} min")
         print(f"  early pins {len(early)} on {len({h[0] for h in early})} turns: {three([h[3] for h in early])} tokens; held before the arrival {three(waits)} s, "
               f"over 30 s {sum(w > 30 for w in waits)}, over 120 s {sum(w > 120 for w in waits)}; ended by " + ", ".join(f"{k} {n}" for k, n in reasons.most_common()))
         print(f"    token-seconds: before the arrival {before / 1e6:,.1f} M ({before / span:,.0f} tokens of the mean level), after it {(area(early) - before) / 1e6:,.1f} M ({(area(early) - before) / span:,.0f})")
         print(f"  the other pins {len(late)}: held {three([h[2] - h[1] for h in late])} s; token-seconds {area(late) / 1e6:,.1f} M ({area(late) / span:,.0f} tokens of the mean level)")
     ```
     Output (seven runs): `jit_p50_d` 3,286 pins, mean level 32,398; early pins 1,182 (1,174 turns), held before arrival 1.1/5.3/41.0 s, 160 over 30 s, ended admit 783 / replaced 335 / abort 64; 164.0 M token-seconds before arrival = 24,573 tokens of the mean level. `fq90` 2,239 pins, mean 16,342; early 419 (410 turns), tokens 1,472/14,656/29,760, held 0.5/3.0/60.2 s, 75 over 30 s, 0 over 120 s, ended admit 270 / abort 82 / replaced 67; 87.6 M = 13,181 tokens; other pins 1,820 (2,586 tokens). `ec_r1` 2,014 pins, mean 5,457; early 367, held 0.5/2.2/12.0 s, 13 over 30 s, 3 over 120 s, admit 272 / replaced 63 / abort 32; 17.8 M = 2,676 tokens. `ec_r13` 327 early, 0.7/2.9/13.3 s, 18 over 30 s, 15.6 M = 2,360. `ec_r13_rep` 293 early, 0.5/3.7/16.4 s, 20 over 30 s, 13.4 M = 2,035. `nofc` 2 early (0.1-0.5 s, log-order artifacts), 0 tokens; mean level 2,910. `jit_exact_d` 1,724 pins, mean 7,130; early 864, held 0.7/1.7/2.4 s, none over 30 s; 26.4 M = 4,041 tokens.
   - **`agent_cache/results/campaign26/compare_ec.out`** (rebuilt, 19 runs, 195 lines; the 18-run rows are unchanged). `fq90` rows: own run 11,204 turns to minute 115, 374 users started, 0 errors, mean TTFT 0.97, median 0.29, p90 2.07, p99 11.9, queue wait 0.46, recomputed 438, SSD pages 505,939, 1,783 turns reading; hardware control decode 36.24/48.47/60.49 ms, first-turn prefill 0.090, SSD 2.30 ms/page; 6,715 shared turns 0.94 = 0.44 + 0.50 | 446 recomputed | landed reads lost 0.7 % | 91 %/93 % of the gain; by stretch 0.36, 0.63, 2.71, 0.70; mechanisms 10,830 own returning turns, 1,750 with a landed read (18,472 tokens each), 67 lost (8.1 % of recompute), 20 shrank, lookups 1.11 (1,058 over one), reads per reading turn 1.215, 55.1/45.8 M tokens evicted; positions 7000 at minute 70.8, 8750 at 89.1, 11000 at 113.2 (leading arms about 67, 85, 108.5); reads and pins 16.2 % reading, staged 613 (582 turns), refused room 625 (371 turns), mean pinned 15,308, unpins `{'admit': 1838, 'abort': 83, 'replaced': 318}`.

Corrected to "82 are given up when the plan times out with the turn still not there, against 32 that rule 1 releases", then fixed the paragraph's wrapping.

   - **Mechanism (measured with `checks/pin_hold.py`):** `fq90` lands about as many reads early as rule 1 (419 against 367 early pins) but they wait longer for their turn (p90 60 s against 12 s; 75 over 30 s against 13; 82 given up on plan timeout against 32 released by rule 1), so before arrival they hold 13.2K tokens of the 44K-token room on average against 2.7K (rules 1+3 2.0-2.4K; exact hints 4.0K; landing at the p10 24.6K). Consequences: 625 read launches refused room against 396, 446 tokens recomputed per turn against 181; the read stall equals rule 1's (0.29 vs 0.30 s per turn), the difference is after admission (0.50 vs 0.44 s).
   - **My reading (labelled as such in the record):** a fixed quantile cannot tell a read whose turn is about to come from one whose turn is a minute out or not coming; moving it from p10 to p90 halves the room held and removes 0.41-0.66 s of the 0.68-0.93 s by which p10 landing trails rule 1; the expected-cost start gets the rest by holding a fifth of the room for about the same number of early reads. This is the deck's argument for rule 1 seen in a run, with rule 1's edge over the better fixed quantile tried at 0.09-0.27 s on one run each. It changes nothing against the rivals that start no read early (they hold no room before arrival and stand where rule 1 does).

@@ 29834 2026-11-13T04:08:49.960Z A len=2046
`ec_r3` (rule 3 alone) finished clean at 10:58 PM ET and lands level with JIT p50: 1.48 s mean TTFT against 1.35–1.60 s. The eviction rule on its own buys nothing on TTFT; the gain of the pricing arms comes from rule 1.

This arm (one run) is JIT p50 with one change: eviction by chance of return within 30 s instead of by next use. With the earlier arms it completes the two-by-two of rules 1 and 3. The table gives mean TTFT on the 6,715 returning turns every run shares, one value per run:

| | Next-use eviction | Rule 3 (evict by return chance) |
|---|---|---|
| JIT p50 read timing | 1.35, 1.48, 1.60 s | 1.48 s |
| Rule 1 (start by expected cost) | 0.67 s | 0.66, 0.78 s |

- **Rule 3:** it moves TTFT by −0.12 to +0.13 s without rule 1 and by −0.02 to +0.10 s with it, so there is no effect in either row. Against the JIT p50 run on the same build the difference is within 0.02 s.
- **Rule 1:** it is worth 0.68–0.93 s under next-use eviction and 0.71–0.83 s under rule 3, with every interval clear of zero.
- **Why rule 3 cannot help here:** the reads still finish early and wait for their turn, holding on average 27.9K of the 44K free tokens of host memory (JIT p50: 24.6K). Memory pinned by a read is outside any eviction rule.
- **SSD traffic:** 4–7 % fewer pages than each JIT p50 run. That is one run, so it needs a repeat before it counts.

For the paper's ablation, rule 3 is not a source of the TTFT gain at this setting; its only candidate benefit is the SSD saving.

Figures in `results/compare_c26_designs/` are redrawn: `timeline_ec_r3.png` is new and `ec_wait_split.png` has a row for the arm. `compare.png` is unchanged because rule 3 alone, like rule 1 alone, is not one of its compared arms; say if you want either added.

`nofc_c128` finished clean at 1:02 AM ET. At 128 users the rival with no gap forecast lands level with the old oracle and the pricing arm: 20.57 s mean TTFT against 20.58 s and 20.57 s.

This arm reads a turn's history from SSD only when the turn arrives, and evicts ended sessions first and queued turns last. Each arm has one run at 128 users, so no order between them counts as shown.

| First 6,900 s, 128 users, 160 GB | Old oracle | JIT exact | JIT p50 | Rules 1+3+4 | No hints | No gap forecast (`nofc_c128`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mean TTFT | 20.58 s | 20.93 s | 20.98 s | 20.57 s | 20.84 s | 20.57 s |
| Turns sent | 15,525 | 15,384 | 15,366 | 15,502 | 15,418 | 15,519 |
| SSD pages read | 1,438K | 998K | 1,195K | 1,049K | 1,153K | 1,122K |

- **TTFT:** every arm that keeps the history of queued turns lands within 0.41 s (2 %) of the others, with or without hints, a gap forecast or a price.
- **Why:** the GPU queue decides the wait. A turn waits about 20 s either way; reading at arrival only moves about a second of that wait from the queue to the read.
- **SSD traffic:** this is what differs. This arm reads 22 % fewer pages than the old oracle and 7 % more than the pricing arm.

So the 128-user setting cannot rank read-start rules. No figure changed: the 128-user figure set holds the five design series, not the rivals.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`agent_cache/results/campaign26/checks/watch_all.sh`** (NEW this window; the watcher to use from now on). Created because `c26_ec/watch.sh` runs its problem checks only for run folders matching `*_NAT160|*custom_L1nat_H70*`, which skips the `_H100` runs. `watch.sh` itself is left unedited. Full content:
     ```bash
     #!/usr/bin/env bash
     # c26_ec/watch.sh with its problem checks widened to every host pool size (that one skips run folders ending in
     # _H100, so the three 100 GB arms ran unchecked). From agent_cache/, on the host:  bash results/campaign26/checks/watch_all.sh
     # Exits on the next arm's end in campaign 26's launcher.log or on a problem: a traceback in the running server's log,
     # client errors, a client that stops writing for 300 s, or the container down.
     L=results/campaign26/launcher.log
     P='C26 .*(DONE|STOP|IDLE)'
     n0=$(grep -cE "$P" "$L")
     while :; do
       n=$(grep -cE "$P" "$L")
       if [ "$n" -gt "$n0" ]; then echo "launcher: $(grep -E "$P" "$L" | tail -1 | cut -c1-240)"; exit 0; fi
       R=$(cat results/.current_run 2>/dev/null)
       case "$R" in *_NAT160|*custom_L1nat_H*)
         if [ -f "results/$R/server.log" ]; then
           tb=$(grep -ac 'Traceback' "results/$R/server.log")
           [ "$tb" -gt 0 ] && { echo "ALERT: $tb tracebacks in results/$R/server.log"; exit 0; }
         fi
         for c in results/$R/client_*/client.jsonl; do
           [ -f "$c" ] || continue
           e=$(grep -ac '"error": "' "$c")
           [ "$e" -gt 0 ] && { echo "ALERT: $e client errors in $c"; exit 0; }
           age=$(( $(date +%s) - $(stat -c %Y "$c") ))
           [ "$age" -gt 300 ] && { echo "ALERT: $c has not grown for $age s"; exit 0; }
         done ;;
       esac
       docker ps --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -q '^sglang_hicache$' || { echo "ALERT: container down"; exit 0; }
       sleep 60
     done
     ```
